What brings you in today?\n\nPatient: I guess… I’ve just been feeling really low lately. Most days it feels like I’m stuck at the bottom of this dark hole, if that makes sense. Even when I’m around people I know, I can’t really shake this heavy, hopeless feeling. I’m sorry, I don’t know if I’m explaining it right.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest or pleasure in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I have. I mean, I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but things I used to really like just don’t… pull me in anymore.\n\nI used to love hiking, painting, going to the makerspace and tinkering with things. It really started after I had that fall on the HVAC job and hurt my back, and then it just felt like things kind of started unraveling from there. It’s been about a year and two months, pretty much since that fall on the HVAC job when I hurt my back. After that, things just kind of… unraveled. Is that okay, like, is that what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel this all started because of the fallout from your back injury?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, I don’t want to blame everything on that, but it really does feel like it started after the fall at the HVAC job, when my back got hurt. Since then, for about a year and two months, things just kind of… started coming apart. Does that answer it?\n\nDoctor: If the problems from your back injury had not happened, do you think you would still have become this depressed?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure, honestly. I keep going back and forth on that. Part of me thinks… maybe not, because things felt more manageable before all that happened. But I don’t know if that’s too simple of an answer. I can’t say for certain what would’ve happened.\n\nDoctor: Before this started after your back injury, had you ever had a separate period in your life when you felt depressed like this for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not like this. I’m not sure what’s normal in that situation.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or on edge a lot during this time?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… maybe some, but I wouldn’t want to overstate it. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: During this time, have you been worrying a lot on most days?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say a lot, exactly… I mean, I do think about things, but I’m not sure it’s “worrying most days” the way you mean. Maybe I’ve had some of that, but I don’t want to make it sound bigger than it is.\n\nDoctor: Have these feelings made it hard for you to keep up with your work?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I wish I could say I’m managing it better, but it has made work hard. I haven’t really been able to keep up with the field work like I used to, or even the business side of jobs. I’ve missed calls and deadlines, and even bills—things I normally handled without even thinking.\n\nI don’t want to sound like I’m making excuses, but it’s gotten to where most days I’m kind of just trying to get through safely at home, and I’ve been pulling back from friends and the makerspace too. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you had thoughts that you’d be better off dead?\n\nPatient: I… yeah. I’m sorry, that’s hard to say. I’ve had thoughts like everyone might be better off if I just didn’t wake up. And sometimes, when the pain and the money stuff piles up, I catch myself thinking about ending it… which scares me. I don’t want to sound dramatic, but that has been there.\n\nDoctor: Are these feelings mostly about the fallout from your back injury?\n\nPatient: Mostly, yeah… I think it all really started after that fall on the HVAC job, when I hurt my back. It really started after I had that fall on the HVAC job and hurt my back, and then it just felt like things kind of started unraveling from there. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling this low pretty much continuously for the past two years?\n\nPatient: Not two full years, no… I don’t think so. It’s been about a year and two months, pretty much since that fall on the HVAC job when I hurt my back. After that, things just kind of… unraveled. Is that okay, like, is that what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel this all started because of the fallout from your back injury?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, I don’t want to blame everything on that, but it really does feel like it started after the fall at the HVAC job, when my back got hurt. Since then, for about a year and two months, things just kind of… started coming apart. Does that answer it?\n\nDoctor: If the problems from your back injury had not happened, do you think you would still have become this depressed?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure, honestly. I keep going back and forth on that. Part of me thinks… maybe not, because things felt more manageable before all that happened. But I don’t know if that’s too simple of an answer. I can’t say for certain what would’ve happened.\n\nDoctor: Before this started after your back injury, had you ever had a separate period in your life when you felt depressed like this for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not like this. I mean, I’ve had rough patches before, like anybody, but I can’t really remember a separate time where it lasted a couple weeks and felt like this. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel your depression has been stronger than most people would expect from what happened with your back injury?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure how to judge that, honestly. I don’t want to make it sound like I’m overreacting or anything. It was a big thing for me, but… whether it’s stronger than what most people would expect, I don’t really know. Maybe? I’m not sure what’s normal in that situation.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or on edge a lot during this time?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… maybe some, but I wouldn’t want to overstate it. I guess I have felt kind of tense or unsettled at times, but I’m not sure if I’d call it anxious a lot. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: During this time, have you been worrying a lot on most days?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say a lot, exactly… I mean, I do think about things, but I’m not sure it’s “worrying most days” the way you mean. Maybe I’ve had some of that, but I don’t want to make it sound bigger than it is.\n\nDoctor: Have these feelings made it hard for you to keep up with your work?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I wish I could say I’m managing it better, but it has made work hard. I haven’t really been able to keep up with the field work like I used to, or even the business side of jobs. I’ve missed calls and deadlines, and even bills—things I normally handled without even thinking.\n\nI don’t want to sound like I’m making excuses, but it’s gotten to where most days I’m kind of just trying to get through safely at home, and I’ve been pulling back from friends and the makerspace too. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you had thoughts that you’d be better off dead?\n\nPatient: I… yeah. I’m sorry, that’s hard to say. I’ve had thoughts like everyone might be better off if I just didn’t wake up. And sometimes, when the pain and the money stuff piles up, I catch myself thinking about ending it… which scares me. I don’t want to sound dramatic, but that has been there.\n\nDoctor: Are these feelings mostly about the fallout from your back injury?\n\nPatient: Mostly, yeah… I think it all really started after that fall on the HVAC job, when I hurt my back. I don’t want to blame everything on one thing, but that’s when things started unraveling for me.\n\nIt’s been… about a year and two months now, I guess. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel depressed even when you’re not thinking about your back injury or the problems that came after it?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, the back injury is a big part of it, but even when I’m not actively thinking about that, I still feel… low.\n\nMost days it’s like I’m sitting at the bottom of some dark hole, if that makes sense. Even if I’m around people I know, I can’t really shake this heavy, hopeless feeling. I try not to make a big deal out of it, but it’s just kind of there.\n\nDoctor: For at least two weeks during this past year, were you feeling depressed nearly all day, almost every day?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. I don’t want to overstate it, but there have definitely been stretches like that where, most days, I feel like I’m at the bottom of this dark hole.\n\nEven when I’m around people, I just can’t really shake that heavy, hopeless feeling. It’s kind of there nearly all day. Is that what you mean?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"

Maybe I’ve had some of that, but I don’t want to make it sound bigger than it is.\n\nDoctor: Have these feelings made it hard for you to keep up with your work?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I wish I could say I’m managing it better, but it has made work hard. I haven’t really been able to keep up with the field work like I used to, or even the business side of jobs. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you had thoughts that you’d be better off dead?\n\nPatient: I… yeah. I’m sorry, that’s hard to say. I don’t want to sound dramatic, but that has been there.\n\nDoctor: Are these feelings mostly about the fallout from your back injury?\n\nPatient: Mostly, yeah… I think it all really started after that fall on the HVAC job, when I hurt my back. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel depressed even when you’re not thinking about your back injury or the problems that came after it?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, the back injury is a big part of it, but even when I’m not actively thinking about that, I still feel… low.\n\nMost days it’s like I’m sitting at the bottom of some dark hole, if that makes sense. Even if I’m around people I know, I can’t really shake this heavy, hopeless feeling. I try not to make a big deal out of it, but it’s just kind of there.\n\nDoctor: For at least two weeks during this past year, were you feeling depressed nearly all day, almost every day?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. What brings you in today?\n\nPatient: I guess… I’ve just been feeling really low lately. Most days it feels like I’m stuck at the bottom of this dark hole, if that makes sense. Even when I’m around people I know, I can’t really shake this heavy, hopeless feeling. I’m sorry, I don’t know if I’m explaining it right.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest or pleasure in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I have. I mean, I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but things I used to really like just don’t… pull me in anymore.\n\nI used to love hiking, painting, going to the makerspace and tinkering with things. Even at work, figuring out a tricky install used to feel kind of satisfying. Now I’ll just stare at my paints or tools and not start anything, or I’ll ignore calls from people because it all feels like too much effort and not really worth it. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this low?\n\nPatient: It’s been… longer than I’d like to admit, I guess. About a year and two months now. It really started after I had that fall on the HVAC job and hurt my back, and then it just felt like things kind of started unraveling from there. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling this low pretty much continuously for the past two years?\n\nPatient: Not two full years, no… I don’t think so. It’s been about a year and two months, pretty much since that fall on the HVAC job when I hurt my back. After that, things just kind of… unraveled. Is that okay, like, is that what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel this all started because of the fallout from your back injury?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, I don’t want to blame everything on that, but it really does feel like it started after the fall at the HVAC job, when my back got hurt. Since then, for about a year and two months, things just kind of… started coming apart. Does that answer it?\n\nDoctor: If the problems from your back injury had not happened, do you think you would still have become this depressed?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure, honestly. I keep going back and forth on that. Part of me thinks… maybe not, because things felt more manageable before all that happened. But I don’t know if that’s too simple of an answer. I can’t say for certain what would’ve happened.\n\nDoctor: Before this started after your back injury, had you ever had a separate period in your life when you felt depressed like this for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not like this. I mean, I’ve had rough patches before, like anybody, but I can’t really remember a separate time where it lasted a couple weeks and felt like this. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel your depression has been stronger than most people would expect from what happened with your back injury?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure how to judge that, honestly. I don’t want to make it sound like I’m overreacting or anything. It was a big thing for me, but… whether it’s stronger than what most people would expect, I don’t really know. Maybe? I’m not sure what’s normal in that situation.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or on edge a lot during this time?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… maybe some, but I wouldn’t want to overstate it. I guess I have felt kind of tense or unsettled at times, but I’m not sure if I’d call it anxious a lot. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: During this time, have you been worrying a lot on most days?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say a lot, exactly… I mean, I do think about things, but I’m not sure it’s “worrying most days” the way you mean. Maybe I’ve had some of that, but I don’t want to make it sound bigger than it is.\n\nDoctor: Have these feelings made it hard for you to keep up with your work?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I wish I could say I’m managing it better, but it has made work hard. I haven’t really been able to keep up with the field work like I used to, or even the business side of jobs. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you had thoughts that you’d be better off dead?\n\nPatient: I… yeah. I’m sorry, that’s hard to say. I’ve had thoughts like everyone might be better off if I just didn’t wake up. And sometimes, when the pain and the money stuff piles up, I catch myself thinking about ending it… which scares me. I don’t want to sound dramatic, but that has been there.\n\nDoctor: Are these feelings mostly about the fallout from your back injury?\n\nPatient: Mostly, yeah… I think it all really started after that fall on the HVAC job, when I hurt my back. I don’t want to blame everything on one thing, but that’s when things started unraveling for me.\n\nIt’s been… about a year and two months now, I guess. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel depressed even when you’re not thinking about your back injury or the problems that came after it?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I’m not sure what’s normal in that situation.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or on edge a lot during this time?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… maybe some, but I wouldn’t want to overstate it. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: During this time, have you been worrying a lot on most days?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say a lot, exactly… I mean, I do think about things, but I’m not sure it’s “worrying most days” the way you mean. Maybe I’ve had some of that, but I don’t want to make it sound bigger than it is.\n\nDoctor: Have these feelings made it hard for you to keep up with your work?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I wish I could say I’m managing it better, but it has made work hard. I haven’t really been able to keep up with the field work like I used to, or even the business side of jobs. I’ve missed calls and deadlines, and even bills—things I normally handled without even thinking.\n\nI don’t want to sound like I’m making excuses, but it’s gotten to where most days I’m kind of just trying to get through safely at home, and I’ve been pulling back from friends and the makerspace too. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you had thoughts that you’d be better off dead?\n\nPatient: I… yeah. I’m sorry, that’s hard to say. I’ve had thoughts like everyone might be better off if I just didn’t wake up. And sometimes, when the pain and the money stuff piles up, I catch myself thinking about ending it… which scares me. I don’t want to sound dramatic, but that has been there.\n\nDoctor: Are these feelings mostly about the fallout from your back injury?\n\nPatient: Mostly, yeah… I think it all really started after that fall on the HVAC job, when I hurt my back. I don’t want to blame everything on one thing, but that’s when things started unraveling for me.\n\nIt’s been… about a year and two months now, I guess. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel depressed even when you’re not thinking about your back injury or the problems that came after it?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. Even if I’m around people I know, I can’t really shake this heavy, hopeless feeling. I try not to make a big deal out of it, but it’s just kind of there.\n\nDoctor: For at least two weeks during this past year, were you feeling depressed nearly all day, almost every day?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. I don’t want to overstate it, but there have definitely been stretches like that where, most days, I feel like I’m at the bottom of this dark hole.\n\nEven when I’m around people, I just can’t really shake that heavy, hopeless feeling. It’s kind of there nearly all day. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: During this time, have you had trouble sleeping?\n\nPatient: Yeah… somewhat. I mean, I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but sleep’s been pretty messed up.\n\nSome nights I’m just lying there for hours, my back hurting and my mind going over bills and everything. Then other times I sleep way too much, like half the day, and I still wake up feeling like I didn’t really rest at all.\n\nIs that the kind of trouble sleeping you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period when you felt unusually up or irritable compared with your normal self?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t think so. Not in a way that stands out to me.\n\nI mean, I get irritated sometimes, sure, but it feels more like normal stress or being worn down, not like I’m suddenly a different person or unusually up. I haven’t really noticed anything like that… unless I’m misunderstanding what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel depressed even on days when your back pain and money problems are not bothering you much?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, the back pain and bills definitely make it worse, but even on days when those aren’t right in my face, I still feel really low.\n\nThat’s kind of why I came in… most days it feels like I’m sitting at the bottom of this dark hole, and even if I’m around people I know, I can’t really shake that heavy, hopeless feeling. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel your depression has been more severe than you would normally expect from what happened with your back injury?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think it has been more than I’d expect, honestly. I don’t want to overstate it, because the back injury has been hard, but this feels deeper than just being upset about that.\n\nIt’s like most days I’m at the bottom of this dark hole, and even if I’m around people I know, I still can’t really shake that heavy, hopeless feeling. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: During this time, have you noticed a big change in your appetite?\n\nPatient: Um… yeah, I guess it has changed. I don’t know if it’s a “big” change, but my eating’s been pretty off.\n\nSome days I’ll just have coffee most of the day because food kind of turns my stomach, and then later at night I’ll pick at whatever’s in the cabinet… not really because I’m hungry, more just… I don’t know. I’m not really tasting it. Is that the kind of thing you mean?"
